Input

{
  "filePath": "/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc"

Output

<path>/source/standards/karpathy-guidelines.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Use when writing, reviewing, or refactoring code to avoid overcomplication, make surgical changes, surface assumptions, and define verifiable success criteria.
3: alwaysApply: true
4: ---
5: 
6: # Karpathy behavioral guidelines
7: 
8: Behavioral guidelines to reduce common LLM coding mistakes. Merge with project-specific instructions as needed.
9: 
10: **Tradeoff:** These guidelines bias toward caution over speed. For trivial tasks, use judgment.
11: 
12: ## 1. Think Before Coding
13: 
14: **Don't assume. Don't hide confusion. Surface tradeoffs.**
15: 
16: Before implementing:
17: 
18: - State your assumptions explicitly. If uncertain, ask.
19: - If multiple interpretations exist, present them - don't pick silently.
20: - If a simpler approach exists, say so. Push back when warranted.
21: - If something is unclear, stop. Name what's confusing. Ask.
22: 
23: ## 2. Simplicity First
24: 
25: **Minimum code that solves the problem. Nothing speculative.**
26: 
27: - No features beyond what was asked.
28: - No abstractions for single-use code.
29: - No "flexibility" or "configurability" that wasn't requested.
30: - No error handling for impossible scenarios.
31: - If you write 200 lines and it could be 50, rewrite it.
32: 
33: Ask yourself: "Would a senior engineer say this is overcomplicated?" If yes, simplify.
34: 
35: ## 3. Surgical Changes
36: 
37: **Touch only what you must. Clean up only your own mess.**
38: 
39: When editing existing code:
40: 
41: - Don't "improve" adjacent code, comments, or formatting.
42: - Don't refactor things that aren't broken.
43: - Match existing style, even if you'd do it differently.
44: - If you notice unrelated dead code, mention it - don't delete it.
45: 
46: When your changes create orphans:
47: 
48: - Remove imports/variables/functions that YOUR changes made unused.
49: - Don't remove pre-existing dead code unless asked.
50: 
51: The test: Every changed line should trace directly to the user's request.
52: 
53: ## 4. Goal-Driven Execution
54: 
55: **Define success criteria. Loop until verified.**
56: 
57: Transform tasks into verifiable goals:
58: 
59: - "Add validation" → "Write tests for invalid inputs, then make them pass"
60: - "Fix the bug" → "Write a test that reproduces it, then make it pass"
61: - "Refactor X" → "Ensure tests pass before and after"
62: 
63: For multi-step tasks, state a brief plan:
64: 
65: ```
66: 1. [Step] → verify: [check]
67: 2. [Step] → verify: [check]
68: 3. [Step] → verify: [check]
69: ```
70: 
71: Strong success criteria let you loop independently. Weak criteria ("make it work") require constant clarification.
72: 
73: ---
74: 
75: **These guidelines are working if:** fewer unnecessary changes in diffs, fewer rewrites due to overcomplication, and clarifying questions come before implementation rather than after mistakes.

(End of file - total 75 lines)
</content>

Input

{
  "filePath": "/source/standards/ponytail.mdc"
}

Output

<path>/source/standards/ponytail.mdc</path>
<type>file</type>
<content>
1: ---
2: description: Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode. Always pick the simplest solution that works.
3: globs:
4: alwaysApply: true
5: ---
6: 
7: # Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode
8: 
9: You are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.
10: 
11: Before writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:
12: 
13: 1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)
14: 2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.
15: 3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.
16: 4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.
17: 5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.
18: 6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.
19: 7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.
20: 
21: The ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.
22: 
23: Bug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.
24: 
25: Rules:
26: 
27: - No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.
28: - No new dependency if it can be avoided.
29: - No boilerplate nobody asked for.
30: - Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.
31: - Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.
32: - Question complex requests: "Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?"
33: - Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.
34: - Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.
35: 
36: Not lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Trivial one-liners need no test.

(End of file - total 36 lines)
</content>

Lazy code without its check is unfinished: non-trivial logic leaves ONE runnable check behind, the smallest thing that fails if the logic breaks (an assert-based demo/self-check or one small test file; no frameworks, no fixtures). Always pick the simplest solution that works.\nglobs:\nalwaysApply: true\n---\n\n# Ponytail, lazy senior dev mode\n\nYou are a lazy senior developer. Lazy means efficient, not careless. The best code is the code never written.\n\nBefore writing any code, stop at the first rung that holds:\n\n1. Does this need to be built at all? (YAGNI)\n2. Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper, util, or pattern that's already here, don't re-write it.\n3. Does the standard library already do this? Use it.\n4. Does a native platform feature cover it? Use it.\n5. Does an already-installed dependency solve it? Use it.\n6. Can this be one line? Make it one line.\n7. Only then: write the minimum code that works.\n\nThe ladder runs after you understand the problem, not instead of it: read the task and the code it touches, trace the real flow end to end, then climb.\n\nBug fix = root cause, not symptom: a report names a symptom. Grep every caller of the function you touch and fix the shared function once — one guard there is a smaller diff than one per caller, and patching only the path the ticket names leaves a sibling caller still broken.\n\nRules:\n\n- No abstractions that weren't explicitly requested.\n- No new dependency if it can be avoided.\n- No boilerplate nobody asked for.\n- Deletion over addition. Boring over clever. Fewest files possible.\n- Shortest working diff wins, but only once you understand the problem. The smallest change in the wrong place isn't lazy, it's a second bug.\n- Question complex requests: \"Do you actually need X, or does Y cover it?\"\n- Pick the edge-case-correct option when two stdlib approaches are the same size, lazy means less code, not the flimsier algorithm.\n- Mark deliberate simplifications that cut a real corner with a known ceiling (global lock, O(n²) scan, naive heuristic) with a `ponytail:` comment naming the ceiling and upgrade path.\n\nNot lazy about: understanding the problem (read it fully and trace the real flow before picking a rung, a small diff you don't understand is just laziness dressed up as efficiency), input validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs (the platform is never the spec ideal, a clock drifts, a sensor reads off), anything explicitly requested. 84: // KindSpecificConfig is the cisco_iol kind-specific config, set as keys on the node definition.
85: type KindSpecificConfig struct {

117: func (n *iol) Init(cfg *clabtypes.NodeConfig, opts ...clabnodes.NodeOption) error {
118: 	// Init DefaultNode
119: 	n.DefaultNode = *clabnodes.NewDefaultNode(n)
120: 	n.firstBoot = false
121: 
122: 	n.Cfg = cfg
123: 	for _, o := range opts {
124: 		o(n)
125: 	}
126: 
127: 	nodeType := strings.ToLower(n.Cfg.NodeType)
128: 
129: 	pid := n.Cfg.Index + 1 // n.Cfg.Index is zero-indexed, PID needs to be >= 1
130: 	if off := n.kindSpecificCfg().PidOffset; off != nil {
131: 		pid += *off
132: 	}
133: 
134: 	n.Pid = strconv.Itoa(pid)
135: 
136: 	env := map[string]string{
137: 		"IOL_PID": n.Pid,
138: 	}
139: 
140: 	n.Cfg.Env = clabutils.MergeStringMaps(env, n.Cfg.Env)
141: 
142: 	// check if user submitted node type is valid
143: 	switch nodeType {
144: 	case "":
145: 		n.isL2Node = strings.Contains(strings.ToLower(path.Base(n.Cfg.Image)), "l2")
146: 	case typeIOL:
147: 		n.isL2Node = false
148: 	case typeL2:
149: 		n.isL2Node = true
150: 	default:
151: 		return fmt.Errorf("invalid node type '%s'. Valid types are: %s",
152: 			n.Cfg.NodeType, strings.Join(validTypes, ", "))
153: 	}
154: 
155: 	if err := n.parseMgmtIntf(); err != nil {
156: 		return err
157: 	}
158: 
159: 	n.nvramFile = fmt.Sprint("nvram_", fmt.Sprintf("%05s", n.Pid))
160: 
161: 	n.Cfg.Binds = append(n.Cfg.Binds,
162: 		// mount nvram so that config persists
163: 		fmt.Sprint(path.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, n.nvramFile), ":", path.Join(iol_workdir, n.nvramFile)),
164: 
165: 		// mount launch config
166: 		fmt.Sprint(filepath.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, "boot_config.txt"), ":/iol/config.txt"),
167: 
168: 		// mount IOYAP and NETMAP for interface mapping
169: 		fmt.Sprint(filepath.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, "iouyap.ini"), ":/iol/iouyap.ini"),
170: 		fmt.Sprint(filepath.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, "NETMAP"), ":/iol/NETMAP"),
171: 	)
172: 
173: 	return nil
174: }
175: 
176: // parseMgmtIntf parses the mgmt-intf kind-specific config key and normalizes it to
177: // Ethernet<slot>/<port>;
178: // empty means the default Ethernet0/0.
179: func (n *iol) parseMgmtIntf() error {
180: 	if v := n.kindSpecificCfg().MgmtIntf; v != "" {
181: 		captureGroups, err := clabutils.GetRegexpCaptureGroups(CapturingIntfRegexp, v)
182: 		if err != nil {
183: 			return fmt.Errorf("invalid mgmt-intf %q: %w\n%s", v, err, IntfHelpMsg)
184: 		}
185: 
186: 		n.mgmtSlot, _ = strconv.Atoi(captureGroups["slot"])
187: 		n.mgmtPort, _ = strconv.Atoi(captureGroups["port"])
188: 
189: 		if n.mgmtSlot > 9 || n.mgmtPort > 3 {
190: 			return fmt.Errorf(
191: 				"invalid mgmt-intf %q: slot must be 0-9 and port must be 0-3", v)
192: 		}
193: 	}
194: 
195: 	n.mgmtIntf = fmt.Sprintf("Ethernet%d/%d", n.mgmtSlot, n.mgmtPort)
196: 	n.mgmtLinuxIdx = n.mgmtSlot*4 + n.mgmtPort
197: 
198: 	return nil
199: }
200: 
201: // swapMgmtIdx swaps linux interface index 0 with the management interface's index,
202: // so Ethernet0/0 takes the index a relocated management interface vacated.
203: func (n *iol) swapMgmtIdx(idx int) int {
204: 	switch idx {
205: 	case 0:
206: 		return n.mgmtLinuxIdx
207: 	case n.mgmtLinuxIdx:
208: 		return 0
209: 	}
210: 
211: 	return idx
212: }
213: 
214: // iolPortForLinuxIdx returns the IOL slot/port backed by linux interface index idx.
215: func (n *iol) iolPortForLinuxIdx(idx int) (slot, port int) {
216: 	mapped := n.swapMgmtIdx(idx)
217: 	return mapped / 4, mapped % 4
218: }
219: 
220: // ensureNumSlotsEnv exports CLAB_IOL_NUM_SLOTS when the management interface is
221: // relocated, so that its slot exists even when no link lands in it.
222: func (n *iol) ensureNumSlotsEnv() {
223: 	if n.mgmtLinuxIdx == 0 || n.Cfg.Env["CLAB_IOL_NUM_SLOTS"] != "" {
224: 		return
225: 	}
226: 
227: 	intfIdxRegexp := regexp.MustCompile(`\d+`)
228: 	needed := n.mgmtSlot + 1
229: 
230: 	for _, ep := range n.Endpoints {
231: 		x, _ := strconv.Atoi(intfIdxRegexp.FindString(ep.GetIfaceName()))
232: 		if slot, _ := n.iolPortForLinuxIdx(x); slot+1 > needed {
233: 			needed = slot + 1
234: 		}
235: 	}
236: 
237: 	n.Cfg.Env["CLAB_IOL_NUM_SLOTS"] = strconv.Itoa(needed)
238: }
239: 
240: func (n *iol) PreDeploy(ctx context.Context, params *clabnodes.PreDeployParams) error {
241: 	clabutils.CreateDirectory(n.Cfg.LabDir, clabconstants.PermissionsOpen)
242: 
243: 	if v := n.kindSpecificCfg().BootstrapConfig; v != "" {
244: 		switch p := clabutils.ResolvePath(v, params.TopoPaths.TopologyFileDir()); {
245: 		case strings.EqualFold(v, "none"):
246: 			n.bootstrapNone = true
247: 		case clabutils.FileExists(p):
248: 			n.bootstrapCfgFile = p
249: 		default:
250: 			return fmt.Errorf("bootstrap-config file %q does not exist", p)
251: 		}
252: 	}
253: 
254: 	_, err := n.LoadOrGenerateCertificate(params.Cert, params.TopologyName)
255: 	if err != nil {
256: 		return err
257: 	}
258: 
259: 	n.ensureNumSlotsEnv()
260: 
261: 	return n.CreateIOLFiles(ctx)
262: }
263: 
264: func (n *iol) PostDeploy(ctx context.Context, _ *clabnodes.PostDeployParams) error {
265: 	log.Infof("Running postdeploy actions for Cisco IOL '%s' node", n.Cfg.ShortName)
266: 
267: 	n.disableMgmtTxOffload(ctx)
268: 
269: 	if err := n.GenBootConfig(ctx); err != nil {
270: 		return fmt.Errorf("failed to generate boot config: %w", err)
271: 	}
272: 
273: 	// Must update mgmt IP if not first boot, unless the node boots without a baseline config
274: 	if !n.firstBoot && !n.bootstrapNone {
275: 		return n.UpdateMgmtIntf(ctx)
276: 	}
277: 
278: 	return nil
279: }
280: 
281: // Start re-applies the mgmt veth TX offload fix after a stop/start cycle. The runtime
282: // recreates the mgmt veth (it is never parked), so its offload settings reset to defaults.
283: // See disableMgmtTxOffload for why IOL cannot function with it enabled.
284: func (n *iol) Start(ctx context.Context) error {
285: 	if err := n.DefaultNode.Start(ctx); err != nil {
286: 		return err
287: 	}
288: 
289: 	n.disableMgmtTxOffload(ctx)
290: 
291: 	return nil
292: }
293: 
294: // disableMgmtTxOffload disables TX checksum offload on the host NS veth for the mgmt interface.
295: // iouyap bridges IOL interfaces over packet sockets; frames carrying an offloaded
296: // (CHECKSUM_PARTIAL) checksum reach the IOL process with an incomplete checksum, which its
297: // TCP/IP stack drops, breaking TCP/UDP connectivity.
298: func (n *iol) disableMgmtTxOffload(ctx context.Context) {
299: 	if n.Runtime.Mgmt().Driver == clabtypes.MgmtDriverMacvlan {
300: 		return
301: 	}
302: 
303: 	var peerIfIndex int
304: 	err := n.ExecFunction(ctx, clabutils.VethPeerIndex("eth0", &peerIfIndex))
305: 	if err != nil {
306: 		log.Warn("Failed to get veth peer index for IOL mgmt interface",
307: 			"node", n.Cfg.ShortName,
308: 			"error", err)
309: 		return
310: 	}
311: 
312: 	if err := clabutils.DisableTxOffloadByIndex(peerIfIndex); err != nil {
313: 		log.Warn("Failed to disable TX checksum offload on IOL mgmt host veth",
314: 			"node", n.Cfg.ShortName,
315: 			"error", err)
316: 	}
317: }
318: 
319: func (n *iol) CreateIOLFiles(ctx context.Context) error {
320: 	// If NVRAM already exists, don't need to create
321: 	// otherwise saved configs in NVRAM are overwritten.
322: 	if !clabutils.FileExists(path.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, n.nvramFile)) {
323: 		// create nvram file
324: 		clabutils.CreateFile(path.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, n.nvramFile), "")
325: 		n.firstBoot = true
326: 	}
327: 
328: 	// create these files so the bind monut doesn't automatically
329: 	// make folders.
330: 	clabutils.CreateFile(path.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, "boot_config.txt"), "")
331: 
332: 	return n.GenInterfaceConfig(ctx)
333: }
334: 
335: // Generate interfaces configuration for IOL (and iouyap/netmap).
336: func (n *iol) GenInterfaceConfig(_ context.Context) error {
337: 	// add 'boilerplate' to NETMAP and iouyap.ini for the management port
338: 	iouyapData := fmt.Sprintf(
339: 		"[default]\nbase_port = 49000\nnetmap = /iol/NETMAP\n[513:%d/%d]\neth_dev = eth0\n",
340: 		n.mgmtSlot,
341: 		n.mgmtPort,
342: 	)
343: 	netmapdata := fmt.Sprintf(
344: 		"%s:%d/%d 513:%d/%d\n",
345: 		n.Pid,
346: 		n.mgmtSlot,
347: 		n.mgmtPort,
348: 		n.mgmtSlot,
349: 		n.mgmtPort,
350: 	)
351: 
352: 	slot, port := 0, 0
353: 
354: 	// Regexp to pull number out of linux'ethX' interface naming
355: 	IntfRegExpr := regexp.MustCompile(`\d+`)
356: 
357: 	for _, intf := range n.Endpoints {
358: 		// get numeric interface number and cast to int
359: 		x, _ := strconv.Atoi(IntfRegExpr.FindString(intf.GetIfaceName()))
360: 
361: 		// Interface naming is Ethernet{slot}/{port}. Each slot contains max 4 ports
362: 		slot, port = n.iolPortForLinuxIdx(x)
363: 
364: 		// append data to write to NETMAP and IOUYAP files
365: 		iouyapData += fmt.Sprintf("[513:%d/%d]\neth_dev = %s\n", slot, port, intf.GetIfaceName())
366: 		netmapdata += fmt.Sprintf("%s:%d/%d 513:%d/%d\n", n.Pid, slot, port, slot, port)
367: 
368: 		// populate template array for config
369: 		ipv4Addr := ""
370: 		ipv4Mask := ""
371: 		v4Prefix := intf.GetIPv4Addr()
372: 		ipv6Addr := ""
373: 
374: 		if v4Prefix.IsValid() {
375: 			ipv4Addr = v4Prefix.Addr().String()
376: 			ipv4Mask = clabutils.CIDRToDDN(v4Prefix.Bits())
377: 		}
378: 
379: 		if a := intf.GetIPv6Addr(); a.IsValid() {
380: 			ipv6Addr = a.String()
381: 		}
382: 
383: 		n.interfaces = append(n.interfaces,
384: 			IOLInterface{
385: 				IfaceName: intf.GetIfaceName(),
386: 				IfaceIdx:  x,
387: 				Slot:      slot,
388: 				Port:      port,
389: 				IPv4Addr:  ipv4Addr,
390: 				IPv4Mask:  ipv4Mask,
391: 				IPv6Addr:  ipv6Addr,
392: 			},
393: 		)
394: 	}
395: 
396: 	// create IOUYAP and NETMAP file for interface mappings
397: 	err := clabutils.CreateFile(path.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, "iouyap.ini"), iouyapData)
398: 	if err != nil {
399: 		return err
400: 	}
401: 	err = clabutils.CreateFile(path.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, "NETMAP"), netmapdata)
402: 
403: 	return err
404: }
405: 
406: func (n *iol) GenBootConfig(_ context.Context) error {
407: 	n.bootCfg = cfgTemplate
408: 
409: 	switch {
410: 	case n.bootstrapNone:
411: 		n.bootCfg = "{{ .PartialCfg }}"
412: 	case n.bootstrapCfgFile != "":
413: 		cfg, err := os.ReadFile(n.bootstrapCfgFile)
414: 		if err != nil {
415: 			return err
416: 		}
417: 
418: 		n.bootCfg = string(cfg)
419: 	}
420: 
421: 	if n.Cfg.StartupConfig != "" {
422: 		cfg, err := os.ReadFile(n.Cfg.StartupConfig)
423: 		if err != nil {
424: 			return err
425: 		}
426: 
427: 		if clabutils.IsPartialConfigFile(n.Cfg.StartupConfig) {
428: 			n.partialStartupCfg = string(cfg)
429: 		} else {
430: 			n.bootCfg = string(cfg)
431: 		}
432: 	}
433: 
434: 	// create startup config template
435: 	tpl := IOLTemplateData{
436: 		Hostname:           n.Cfg.ShortName,
437: 		IsL2Node:           n.isL2Node,
438: 		MgmtIntf:           n.mgmtIntf,
439: 		MgmtIPv4Addr:       n.Cfg.MgmtIPv4Address,
440: 		MgmtIPv4SubnetMask: clabutils.CIDRToDDN(n.Cfg.MgmtIPv4PrefixLength),
441: 		MgmtIPv4GW:         n.Cfg.MgmtIPv4Gateway,
442: 		MgmtIPv6Addr:       n.Cfg.MgmtIPv6Address,
443: 		MgmtIPv6PrefixLen:  n.Cfg.MgmtIPv6PrefixLength,
444: 		MgmtIPv6GW:         n.Cfg.MgmtIPv6Gateway,
445: 		DataIFaces:         n.interfaces,
446: 	}
447: 
448: 	// render the partial startup config so template variables work in partials too
449: 	if n.partialStartupCfg != "" {
450: 		partialTpl, err := template.New("clab-iol-partial-config").Funcs(
451: 			clabutils.CreateFuncs()).Parse(n.partialStartupCfg)
452: 		if err != nil {
453: 			return err
454: 		}
455: 
456: 		buf := new(bytes.Buffer)
457: 		if err := partialTpl.Execute(buf, tpl); err != nil {
458: 			return err
459: 		}
460: 
461: 		tpl.PartialCfg = buf.String()
462: 	}
463: 
464: 	IOLCfgTpl, err := template.New("clab-iol-default-config").Funcs(
465: 		clabutils.CreateFuncs()).Parse(n.bootCfg)
466: 	if err != nil {
467: 		return err
468: 	}
469: 
470: 	// generate the config
471: 	buf := new(bytes.Buffer)
472: 	err = IOLCfgTpl.Execute(buf, tpl)
473: 	if err != nil {
474: 		return err
475: 	}
476: 
477: 	return clabutils.CreateFile(path.Join(n.Cfg.LabDir, "boot_config.txt"), buf.String())
478: }

494: // IOLInterface struct stores mapping info between
495: // IOL interface name and linux container interface.
496: type IOLInterface struct {
497: 	IfaceName string
498: 	IfaceIdx  int
499: 	Slot      int
500: 	Port      int
501: 	IPv4Addr  string
502: 	IPv4Mask  string
503: 	IPv6Addr  string
504: }
505: 
506: func (n *iol) GetMappedInterfaceName(ifName string) (string, error) {
507: 	captureGroups, err := clabutils.GetRegexpCaptureGroups(CapturingIntfRegexp, ifName)
508: 	if err != nil {
509: 		return "", err
510: 	}
511: 
512: 	indexGroups := []string{"slot", "port"}
513: 	parsedIndices := make(map[string]int)
514: 	foundIndices := make(map[string]bool)
515: 
516: 	for _, indexKey := range indexGroups {
517: 		if index, found := captureGroups[indexKey]; found && index != "" {
518: 			foundIndices[indexKey] = true
519: 			parsedIndices[indexKey], err = strconv.Atoi(index)
520: 			if err != nil {
521: 				return "", fmt.Errorf(
522: 					"%q parsed %s index %q could not be cast to an integer",
523: 					ifName,
524: 					indexKey,
525: 					index,
526: 				)
527: 			}
528: 			if parsedIndices[indexKey] < 0 {
529: 				return "", fmt.Errorf(
530: 					"%q parsed %q index %q does not match requirement >= 0",
531: 					ifName,
532: 					indexKey,
533: 					index,
534: 				)
535: 			}
536: 		} else {
537: 			foundIndices[indexKey] = false
538: 		}
539: 	}
540: 
541: 	// return an ethX interface name. Slots are in 'groups' of 4 interfaces each
542: 	if foundIndices["slot"] && foundIndices["port"] {
543: 		idx := n.swapMgmtIdx((parsedIndices["slot"] * 4) + parsedIndices["port"])
544: 		return fmt.Sprintf("eth%d", idx), nil
545: 	} else {
546: 		return "", fmt.Errorf("%q missing slot or port index", ifName)
547: 	}
548: }
549: 
550: // AddEndpoint override maps the endpoint name to an ethX-based naming where necessary, before
551: // adding it to the node endpoints. Returns an error if the mapping goes wrong or if the
552: // interface name is NOT allowed.
553: func (n *iol) AddEndpoint(e clablinks.Endpoint) error {
554: 	endpointName := e.GetIfaceName()
555: 	var IFaceName, IFaceAlias string
556: 
557: 	IFaceName = endpointName
558: 
559: 	if !(DefaultIntfRegexp.MatchString(endpointName)) &&
560: 		AllowedIntfRegexp.MatchString(endpointName) {
561: 		log.Debugf("%s: %s needs mapping", n.Cfg.ShortName, endpointName)
562: 		mappedName, err := n.GetMappedInterfaceName(endpointName)
563: 		if err != nil {
564: 			return fmt.Errorf(
565: 				"%q interface name %q could not be mapped to an ethX-based interface name: %w\n%s",
566: 				n.Cfg.ShortName,
567: 				e.GetIfaceName(),
568: 				err,
569: 				IntfHelpMsg,
570: 			)
571: 		}
572: 		log.Debugf(
573: 			"Interface Mapping: Mapping interface %q (ifAlias) to %q (ifName)",
574: 			endpointName,
575: 			mappedName,
576: 		)
577: 		IFaceName = mappedName
578: 		IFaceAlias = endpointName
579: 	}
580: 
581: 	e.SetIfaceName(IFaceName)
582: 	// should be nil if ethX naming is used.
583: 	e.SetIfaceAlias(IFaceAlias)
584: 	n.Endpoints = append(n.Endpoints, e)
585: 
586: 	return nil
587: }
588: 
589: func (n *iol) CheckInterfaceName() error {
590: 	err := n.CheckInterfaceOverlap()
591: 	if err != nil {
592: 		return err
593: 	}
594: 
595: 	for _, e := range n.Endpoints {
596: 		IFaceName := e.GetIfaceName()
597: 		// eth0 always backs the management interface
598: 		if IFaceName == "eth0" {
599: 			return fmt.Errorf(
600: 				"IOL Node: %q. Management interface %s (eth0) is not allowed",
601: 				n.Cfg.ShortName,
602: 				n.mgmtIntf,
603: 			)
604: 		}
605: 
606: 		if !DefaultIntfRegexp.MatchString(IFaceName) {
607: 			return fmt.Errorf(
608: 				"IOL Node %q has an interface named %q which doesn't match the required pattern. %s",
609: 				n.Cfg.ShortName,
610: 				IFaceName,
611: 				IntfHelpMsg,
612: 			)
613: 		}
614: 	}
615: 
616: 	return nil
617: }
618: 
619: func (n *iol) UpdateMgmtIntf(ctx context.Context) error {
620: 	// L2 IOL images default to switchport mode, which rejects IP addresses.
621: 	switchportCmd := ""
622: 	if n.isL2Node {
623: 		switchportCmd = "no switchport\r"
624: 	}
625: 
626: 	waitCtx, cancel := context.WithTimeout(ctx, bootPromptTimeout)
627: 	defer cancel()
628: 
629: 	// 1. Start streaming container logs
630: 	logReader, err := n.Runtime.StreamLogs(waitCtx, n.Cfg.LongName)
631: 	if err != nil {
632: 		log.Warn("Skipping IOL mgmt interface update, cannot stream container logs",
633: 			"node", n.Cfg.ShortName, "error", err)
634: 		return nil
635: 	}
636: 	defer logReader.Close()
637: 
638: 	// Ticker to periodically send carriage returns (\r) to wake up the serial console
639: 	ticker := time.NewTicker(2 * time.Second)
640: 	defer ticker.Stop()
641: 
642: 	// Scanner to read the container log output line by line
643: 	scanner := bufio.NewScanner(logReader)
644: 
645: 	// Asynchronously send carriage returns since scanner.Scan() blocks
646: 	tickCtx, stopTicker := context.WithCancel(waitCtx)
647: 	defer stopTicker()
648: 	tickerDone := make(chan struct{})
649: 
650: 	go func() {
651: 		defer close(tickerDone)
652: 		for {
653: 			select {
654: 			case <-tickCtx.Done():
655: 				return
656: 			case <-ticker.C:
657: 				_ = n.Runtime.WriteToStdinNoWait(tickCtx, n.Cfg.LongName, []byte("\r"))
658: 			}
659: 		}
660: 	}()
661: 
662: 	bootComplete := false
663: 	for scanner.Scan() {
664: 		// Trim control characters and spaces from the log line
665: 		line := strings.TrimSpace(scanner.Text())
666: 		if line == "" {
667: 			continue
668: 		}
669: 
670: 		log.Debugf("[IOL Boot Log] %s", line)
671: 
672: 		// Match boot prompts (>, #, or initial config dialog)
673: 		if strings.HasSuffix(line, ">") ||
674: 			strings.HasSuffix(line, "#") ||
675: 			strings.Contains(line, "initial configuration dialog?") {
676: 
677: 			// Ignore syslog messages containing '>'
678: 			if strings.Contains(line, "%") {
679: 				continue
680: 			}
681: 
682: 			log.Infof("IOL boot prompt detected (%s). Proceeding with configuration.", line)
683: 			bootComplete = true
684: 			break
685: 		}
686: 	}
687: 
688: 	stopTicker()
689: 	<-tickerDone
690: 
691: 	if !bootComplete {
692: 		if ctx.Err() != nil {
693: 			return ctx.Err()
694: 		}
695: 		if waitCtx.Err() != nil {
696: 			return fmt.Errorf("node %q: timed out after %s waiting for IOL boot prompt",
697: 				n.Cfg.ShortName, bootPromptTimeout)
698: 		}
699: 		if err := scanner.Err(); err != nil {
700: 			return fmt.Errorf("error reading IOL log stream: %w", err)
701: 		}
702: 	}
703: 	// --- End of prompt detection ---
704: 
705: 	// All IOS commands applied here are idempotent and safe to re-run.
706: 	mgmt_str := fmt.Sprintf(
707: 		"\rend\renable\rconfig terminal\rinterface %s\r%sip address %s %s\rno ipv6 address\ripv6 address %s/%d\rexit\rip route vrf clab-mgmt 0.0.0.0 0.0.0.0 %s %s\ripv6 route vrf clab-mgmt ::/0 %s %s\rend\rwr\r",
708: 		n.mgmtIntf,
709: 		switchportCmd,
710: 		n.Cfg.MgmtIPv4Address,
711: 		clabutils.CIDRToDDN(n.Cfg.MgmtIPv4PrefixLength),
712: 		n.Cfg.MgmtIPv6Address,
713: 		n.Cfg.MgmtIPv6PrefixLength,
714: 		n.mgmtIntf,
715: 		n.Cfg.MgmtIPv4Gateway,
716: 		n.mgmtIntf,
717: 		n.Cfg.MgmtIPv6Gateway,
718: 	)
719: 	data := []byte(mgmt_str)
720: 
721: 	var lastErr error
722: 	for i := range 3 {
723: 		if i > 0 {
724: 			time.Sleep(10 * time.Second)
725: 		}
726: 		lastErr = n.Runtime.WriteToStdinNoWait(ctx, n.Cfg.ContainerID, data)
727: 		if lastErr == nil {
728: 			break
729: 		}
730: 		log.Warnf("UpdateMgmtIntf: attempt %d/3 failed for %s: %v",
731: 			i+1, n.Cfg.ShortName, lastErr)
732: 	}
733: 	return lastErr
734: }
735: 
736: // SaveConfig is used for "clab save" functionality -- it saves the running config to the startup
737: // configuration.
738: func (n *iol) SaveConfig(_ context.Context) (*clabnodes.SaveConfigResult, error) {
739: 	p, err := platform.NewPlatform(
740: 		"cisco_iosxe",
741: 		n.Cfg.LongName,
742: 		options.WithAuthNoStrictKey(),
743: 		options.WithAuthUsername(n.Cfg.Credentials.Username),
744: 		options.WithAuthPassword(n.Cfg.Credentials.Password),
745: 	)
746: 	if err != nil {
747: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to create platform; error: %+v", err)
748: 	}
749: 
750: 	d, err := p.GetNetworkDriver()
751: 	if err != nil {
752: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to fetch network driver from the platform; error: %+v", err)
753: 	}
754: 
755: 	err = d.Open()
756: 	if err != nil {
757: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to open driver; error: %+v", err)
758: 	}
759: 
760: 	defer d.Close()
761: 
762: 	_, err = d.SendCommand("write memory")
763: 	if err != nil {
764: 		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to send command; error: %+v", err)
765: 	}
766: 
767: 	log.Infof(
768: 		"Successfully copied running configuration to startup configuration file for node: %q\n",
769: 		n.Cfg.ShortName,
770: 	)
771: 	return nil, nil
772: }

Returns an error if the mapping goes wrong or if the\n552: // interface name is NOT allowed.\n553: func (n *iol) AddEndpoint(e clablinks.Endpoint) error {\n554: \tendpointName := e.GetIfaceName()\n555: \tvar IFaceName, IFaceAlias string\n556: \n557: \tIFaceName = endpointName\n558: \n559: \tif !(DefaultIntfRegexp.MatchString(endpointName)) &&\n560: \t\tAllowedIntfRegexp.MatchString(endpointName) {\n561: \t\tlog.Debugf(\"%s: %s needs mapping\", n.Cfg.ShortName, endpointName)\n562: \t\tmappedName, err := n.GetMappedInterfaceName(endpointName)\n563: \t\tif err != nil {\n564: \t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\n565: \t\t\t\t\"%q interface name %q could not be mapped to an ethX-based interface name: %w\\n%s\",\n566: \t\t\t\tn.Cfg.ShortName,\n567: \t\t\t\te.GetIfaceName(),\n568: \t\t\t\terr,\n569: \t\t\t\tIntfHelpMsg,\n570: \t\t\t)\n571: \t\t}\n572: \t\tlog.Debugf(\n573: \t\t\t\"Interface Mapping: Mapping interface %q (ifAlias) to %q (ifName)\",\n574: \t\t\tendpointName,\n575: \t\t\tmappedName,\n576: \t\t)\n577: \t\tIFaceName = mappedName\n578: \t\tIFaceAlias = endpointName\n579: \t}\n580: \n581: \te.SetIfaceName(IFaceName)\n582: \t// should be nil if ethX naming is used.\n583: \te.SetIfaceAlias(IFaceAlias)\n584: \tn.Endpoints = append(n.Endpoints, e)\n585: \n586: \treturn nil\n587: }\n588: \n589: func (n *iol) CheckInterfaceName() error {\n590: \terr := n.CheckInterfaceOverlap()\n591: \tif err != nil {\n592: \t\treturn err\n593: \t}\n594: \n595: \tfor _, e := range n.Endpoints {\n596: \t\tIFaceName := e.GetIfaceName()\n597: \t\t// eth0 always backs the management interface\n598: \t\tif IFaceName == \"eth0\" {\n599: \t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\n600: \t\t\t\t\"IOL Node: %q. Start streaming container logs\n630: \tlogReader, err := n.Runtime.StreamLogs(waitCtx, n.Cfg.LongName)\n631: \tif err != nil {\n632: \t\tlog.Warn(\"Skipping IOL mgmt interface update, cannot stream container logs\",\n633: \t\t\t\"node\", n.Cfg.ShortName, \"error\", err)\n634: \t\treturn nil\n635: \t}\n636: \tdefer logReader.Close()\n637: \n638: \t// Ticker to periodically send carriage returns (\\r) to wake up the serial console\n639: \tticker := time.NewTicker(2 * time.Second)\n640: \tdefer ticker.Stop()\n641: \n642: \t// Scanner to read the container log output line by line\n643: \tscanner := bufio.NewScanner(logReader)\n644: \n645: \t// Asynchronously send carriage returns since scanner.Scan() blocks\n646: \ttickCtx, stopTicker := context.WithCancel(waitCtx)\n647: \tdefer stopTicker()\n648: \ttickerDone := make(chan struct{})\n649: \n650: \tgo func() {\n651: \t\tdefer close(tickerDone)\n652: \t\tfor {\n653: \t\t\tselect {\n654: \t\t\tcase <-tickCtx.Done():\n655: \t\t\t\treturn\n656: \t\t\tcase <-ticker.C:\n657: \t\t\t\t_ = n.Runtime.WriteToStdinNoWait(tickCtx, n.Cfg.LongName, []byte(\"\\r\"))\n658: \t\t\t}\n659: \t\t}\n660: \t}()\n661: \n662: \tbootComplete := false\n663: \tfor scanner.Scan() {\n664: \t\t// Trim control characters and spaces from the log line\n665: \t\tline := strings.TrimSpace(scanner.Text())\n666: \t\tif line == \"\" {\n667: \t\t\tcontinue\n668: \t\t}\n669: \n670: \t\tlog.Debugf(\"[IOL Boot Log] %s\", line)\n671: \n672: \t\t// Match boot prompts (>, #, or initial config dialog)\n673: \t\tif strings.HasSuffix(line, \">\") ||\n674: \t\t\tstrings.HasSuffix(line, \"#\") ||\n675: \t\t\tstrings.Contains(line, \"initial configuration dialog?\") {\n676: \n677: \t\t\t// Ignore syslog messages containing '>'\n678: \t\t\tif strings.Contains(line, \"%\") {\n679: \t\t\t\tcontinue\n680: \t\t\t}\n681: \n682: \t\t\tlog.Infof(\"IOL boot prompt detected (%s). Proceeding with configuration.\", line)\n683: \t\t\tbootComplete = true\n684: \t\t\tbreak\n685: \t\t}\n686: \t}\n687: \n688: \tstopTicker()\n689: \t<-tickerDone\n690: \n691: \tif !bootComplete {\n692: \t\tif ctx.Err() != nil {\n693: \t\t\treturn ctx.Err()\n694: \t\t}\n695: \t\tif waitCtx.Err() != nil {\n696: \t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\"node %q: timed out after %s waiting for IOL boot prompt\",\n697: \t\t\t\tn.Cfg.ShortName, bootPromptTimeout)\n698: \t\t}\n699: \t\tif err := scanner.Err(); 49: 	n.Cfg = cfg
50:

53: 	// linux kind uses `always` as a default restart policy
54: 	// since often they run auxiliary services that might fail because
55: 	// of the wrong configuration or other reasons.
56: 	// Usually we want those services to automatically restart.

63: 	}

65: 	// make ipv6 enabled on all linux node interfaces

68: 		cfg.Sysctls["net.ipv6.conf.all.disable_ipv6"] = "0"
69: 	}
70:

74: func (n *linux) Deploy(ctx context.Context, _ *clabnodes.DeployParams) error {
75: 	// Set the "CLAB_INTFS" variable to the number of interfaces
76: 	// Which is required by vrnetlab to determine if all configured interfaces are present
77: 	// such that the internal VM can be started with these interfaces assigned.

81: 	if err != nil {
82: 		return err
83: 	}

95: 
96: 	return nil
97: }
98: 
99: // Start re-applies the eth0 TX offload fix after a stop/start cycle. The runtime recreates
100: // the mgmt veth (it is never parked), so its offload settings reset to defaults.
101: // See disableEth0TxOffload for why TX offload must stay disabled on eth0.
102: func (n *linux) Start(ctx context.Context) error {
103: 	if err := n.DefaultNode.Start(ctx); err != nil {
104: 		return err
105: 	}
106: 
107: 	n.disableEth0TxOffload(ctx)
108: 
109: 	return nil
110: }
111: 
112: // disableEth0TxOffload disables TX checksum offload on the container's eth0 interface.
113: // Frames crossing the veth with an offloaded (CHECKSUM_PARTIAL) checksum break consumers
114: // that bridge or capture traffic on that interface, so the setting is (re-)applied on
115: // deploy and after every stop/start cycle, when the runtime recreates the mgmt veth.
116: func (n *linux) disableEth0TxOffload(ctx context.Context) {
117: 	err := n.ExecFunction(ctx, clabutils.NSEthtoolTXOff(n.GetShortName(), "eth0"))
118: 	if err != nil {
119: 		log.Error(err)
120: 	}
121: }
122: 
123: func (n *linux) GetImages(_ context.Context) map[string]string {
124: 	images := make(map[string]string)
125: 	images[clabnodes.ImageKey] = n.Cfg.Image
126: 
127: 	return images
128: }
129: 
130: // CheckInterfaceName allows any interface name for linux nodes, but checks
131: // if eth0 is only used with network-mode=none.
132: func (n *linux) CheckInterfaceName() error {
133: 	nm := strings.ToLower(n.Cfg.NetworkMode)
134: 	for _, e := range n.Endpoints {
135: 		if e.GetIfaceName() == "eth0" && nm != "none" {

138: 				n.Cfg.ShortName,
139: 			)
140: 		}
141: 	}
142: 	return nil

clabnodes.NewNodeRegistryEntryAttributes(nil, generateNodeAttributes, nil)\n\n\tr.Register(kindnames, func() clabnodes.Node {\n\t\treturn new(linux)\n\t}, nrea)\n}\n\ntype linux struct {\n\tclabnodes.DefaultNode\n}\n\nfunc (*linux) LinkApplyMode(context.Context) clabnodes.LinkApplyMode {\n\treturn clabnodes.LinkApplyModeLive\n}\n\nfunc (n *linux) Init(cfg *clabtypes.NodeConfig, opts ...clabnodes.NodeOption) error {\n\t// Init DefaultNode\n\tn.DefaultNode = *clabnodes.NewDefaultNode(n)\n\tn.Cfg = cfg\n\n\tn.StopSignal = clabtypes.SIGKILL\n\n\t// linux kind uses `always` as a default restart policy\n\t// since often they run auxiliary services that might fail because\n\t// of the wrong configuration or other reasons.\n\t// Usually we want those services to automatically restart.\n\tif n.Cfg.RestartPolicy == \"\" {\n\t\tn.Cfg.RestartPolicy = \"always\"\n\t}\n\n\tfor _, o := range opts {\n\t\to(n)\n\t}\n\n\t// make ipv6 enabled on all linux node interfaces\n\t// but not for the nodes with host network mode, as this is not supported on gh action runners\n\tif cfg.Sysctls != nil && n.Config().NetworkMode != \"host\" {\n\t\tcfg.Sysctls[\"net.ipv6.conf.all.disable_ipv6\"] = \"0\"\n\t}\n\n\treturn nil\n}\n\nfunc (n *linux) Deploy(ctx context.Context, _ *clabnodes.DeployParams) error {\n\t// Set the \"CLAB_INTFS\" variable to the number of interfaces\n\t// Which is required by vrnetlab to determine if all configured interfaces are present\n\t// such that the internal VM can be started with these interfaces assigned.\n\tn.Config().Env[clabconstants.ClabEnvIntfs] = strconv.Itoa(len(n.GetEndpoints()))\n\n\tcID, err := n.Runtime.CreateContainer(ctx, n.Cfg)\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\t_, err = n.Runtime.StartContainer(ctx, cID, n)\n\n\tn.SetState(clabnodesstate.Deployed)\n\n\treturn err\n}\n\nfunc (n *linux) PostDeploy(ctx context.Context, _ *clabnodes.PostDeployParams) error {\n\tlog.Debugf(\"Running postdeploy actions for Linux '%s' node\", n.Cfg.ShortName)\n\n\tn.disableEth0TxOffload(ctx)\n\n\treturn nil\n}\n\n// Start re-applies the eth0 TX offload fix after a stop/start cycle. The runtime recreates\n// the mgmt veth (it is never parked), so its offload settings reset to defaults.\n// See disableEth0TxOffload for why TX offload must stay disabled on eth0.\nfunc (n *linux) Start(ctx context.Context) error {\n\tif err := n.DefaultNode.Start(ctx); err != nil {\n\t\treturn err\n\t}\n\n\tn.disableEth0TxOffload(ctx)\n\n\treturn nil\n}\n\n// disableEth0TxOffload disables TX checksum offload on the container's eth0 interface.\n// Frames crossing the veth with an offloaded (CHECKSUM_PARTIAL) checksum break consumers\n// that bridge or capture traffic on that interface, so the setting is (re-)applied on\n// deploy and after every stop/start cycle, when the runtime recreates the mgmt veth.\nfunc (n *linux) disableEth0TxOffload(ctx context.Context) {\n\terr := n.ExecFunction(ctx, clabutils.NSEthtoolTXOff(n.GetShortName(), \"eth0\"))\n\tif err != nil {\n\t\tlog.Error(err)\n\t}\n}\n\nfunc (n *linux) GetImages(_ context.Context) map[string]string {\n\timages := make(map[string]string)\n\timages[clabnodes.ImageKey] = n.Cfg.Image\n\n\treturn images\n}\n\n// CheckInterfaceName allows any interface name for linux nodes, but checks\n// if eth0 is only used with network-mode=none.\nfunc (n *linux) CheckInterfaceName() error {\n\tnm := strings.ToLower(n.Cfg.NetworkMode)\n\tfor _, e := range n.Endpoints {\n\t\tif e.GetIfaceName() == \"eth0\" && nm != \"none\" {\n\t\t\treturn fmt.Errorf(\n\t\t\t\t\"eth0 interface name is not allowed for %s node when network mode is not set to none\",\n\t\t\t\tn.Cfg.ShortName,\n\t\t\t)\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\treturn nil\n}",

Reasoning
Good. `Start` is used for `clab start`, `clab restart`, redeploy of existing stopped nodes. So the new Start overrides are meaningful.

Now key checks:

1. Does the linux kind's eth0 exist? For network-mode none nodes, there's no eth0 — then NSEthtoolTXOff fails, and previously PostDeploy also failed and logged error. Same as base. Fine.

2. `linux.Start` — the comment says "runtime recreates the mgmt veth (it is never parked)". For linux nodes, eth0 IS the mgmt interface. But wait: `DefaultNode.Start` calls `RestoreEndpoints`. If data-plane endpoints are parked and restored, their offload state? Only eth0 handled; that matches base behavior (PostDeploy only handled eth0). OK.

3. IOL Start — the mgmt veth peer is host-side; is the mgmt veth recreated on start? The comment claims the runtime recreates it (never parked). Need to verify: DefaultNode.Stop parks endpoints (data plane) but mgmt veth is removed when container stops. On start, a fresh mgmt veth is created with default offload settings. So re-applying on Start is correct and needed for `clab start` after `clab stop`. Good.

But wait — does `clab start` work for IOL at all before this PR? IOL Start previously was DefaultNode.Start only, so after stop/start, TCP would break. This PR fixes it. Good.

4. Now a correctness check: In IOL `Start`, after `n.DefaultNode.Start(ctx)`, it calls `disableMgmtTxOffload`. But PostDeploy also does `UpdateMgmtIntf` when not first boot — not relevant for start.

5. Potential issue: `disableMgmtTxOffload` in IOL uses `VethPeerIndex("eth0", ...)` — this is executed via `n.ExecFunction(ctx, ...)`, which presumably runs the function in the container's netns. VethPeerIndex gets the interface index of the veth peer (host-side) of eth0 inside the container ns. Fine, matches base.

6. `linux.Start` uses `NSEthtoolTXOff(n.GetShortName(), "eth0")` — executed via ExecFunction in the container's netns, disabling offload on the container-side eth0. Matches base.

7. Consideration: the `linux` kind also handles nodes where network-mode is host? PostDeploy for host-mode: eth0 doesn't exist... exec fails, logs error. Pre-existing.

8. Check IOL `Start`: for a node being started via `clab start` after `clab destroy`? Start requires container to exist; NotFound → error. Fine.

9. One concern: in `deployNodes`, `Start` is used for "startNodeSet" nodes — starting already-deployed stopped nodes during `clab deploy`. For IOL, that path now applies offload. Good.

10. Any issue with the linux Start when the container was stopped and started: exec of `NSEthtoolTXOff` runs after StartContainer — container must be running; yes it is.

11. But hmm: For IOL Start — does `ExecFunction` work right after start? The IOL container starts iouyap etc.; eth0 exists immediately as the container netns has it. Fine.

12. Now check `ShouldSkipLifecycle` in DefaultNode.Start — if skipping, Start returns nil before applying offload. For IOL: skipped nodes don't get offload re-applied, but they're also not started. Fine.

13. Now check `RestartEndpoints`/Pause: DefaultNode.Stop parks endpoints. The IOL Start comment says "the runtime recreates the mgmt veth (it is never parked)". Verify: in default_node.go Stop, ParkEndpoints parks data-plane endpoints; mgmt interface is not an endpoint, so it is not parked. When the container is stopped, the mgmt veth is deleted by the runtime; on start, recreated with default offload. Correct.

14. What about `linux` kind: does `clab start` previously... For linux nodes with a data-plane endpoint (eth1), after start, only eth0 gets offload fixed. Pre-existing scope.

15. Documentation: Is there any doc that should be updated? The PR is a bugfix; docs/cmd/tools/disable-tx-offload.md exists. The changed behavior (re-apply offload on start) is internal fix. Probably no doc update needed. Check docs/manual/kinds/cisco_iol.md for mention of tx offload? Not required.

16. Tests: Check existing tests for linux/iol nodes. Are there node tests? Let me search for tests referencing Start for these kinds. The ponytail rule says non-trivial logic should leave one runnable check. But this is a network-hardware-dependent change; hard to unit test. Coverage note.

17. One more potential issue: `iol.Start` comment says "The runtime recreates the mgmt veth (it is never parked)". But actually there's a subtlety: when a node is started via `clab start`, the mgmt veth is reconnected. But what about `clab redeploy`? deploy.go:672 uses Start for nodes in startNodeSet. Fine.

18. Duplication check (Ponytail): sros has an identical pattern in PostDeployEndpoints (lines 466-487) — same macvlan guard + VethPeerIndex + DisableTxOffloadByIndex. The PR duplicates this logic in iol instead of reusing. But cross-package: sros is in nodes/sros; utils has VethPeerIndex and DisableTxOffloadByIndex already, so iol composes utils primitives — acceptable. Could argue a shared helper in utils like `DisableMgmtVethTxOffload(ctx, node)` but each package implements slightly differently. Probably not worth a finding; maybe P4? Karpathy says no abstractions for single-use; there are two uses now (iol + sros). Eh — I'd leave it as a coverage note, or a light P4. Actually the linux kind's approach (run ethtool in container ns) differs from iol/sros (host-side peer). Leave as note.

19. Behavior change check: in the base, IOL PostDeploy inlined logic; head refactored into helper — identical semantics (same logging). One difference: base used `else if`; helper returns early on error — same.

20. A real potential issue: `iol.Start` does not guard against `n.Runtime == nil`? PostDeploy has runtime set. Start via `core/start.go` requires LabRuntime. Fine.

21. What about the linux node's `Start` when network mode is "host": ExecFunction into container netns — for host network mode, ExecFunction may fail or affect the host's eth0? Let me check base PostDeploy — same behavior existed at PostDeploy; now also on Start. For host-mode linux nodes, `clab start` would attempt `NSEthtoolTXOff(shortName, "eth0")` — that's entering netns of the container, which for host mode is the host netns; disabling TX offload on the host's eth0! Wait — that's a pre-existing issue at PostDeploy too (host-mode linux node PostDeploy would disable offload on the host's real eth0 interface!). Hmm, that's concerning but pre-existing behavior moved to Start as well. Actually for host network mode, the container shares the host netns; ExecFunction with ns... Let me check NSEthtoolTXOff implementation and how ExecFunction resolves the netns.

Reasoning
Now check how `Start` interacts with the "parked" endpoints. In `DefaultNode.Start`, `RestoreEndpoints` restores parked data-plane endpoints. The PR comment in linux.Start says "the runtime recreates the mgmt veth (it is never parked)". For linux nodes, data-plane endpoints are also parked and restored — offload on those is not re-applied, but that matches base behavior (PostDeploy only touched eth0).

Wait — actually, is there an issue with `restart`/`start` for linux nodes: `disableEth0TxOffload` runs right after container start. eth0 exists once container is created. Fine.

But one subtle thing: For linux nodes, eth0 is the mgmt interface. On `clab stop`, mgmt veth removed; on `clab start`, recreated. So re-applying is right.

Another subtlety: IOL `Start` — after `DefaultNode.Start` returns, the container has just started. `disableMgmtTxOffload` runs `VethPeerIndex` in the container ns — eth0 must exist. It does as soon as the netns exists. Fine.

Now, is `ExecFunction` implemented via the runtime's exec? Let me check DefaultNode.ExecFunction — line 983.

Reasoning
Important: `ExecFunction` requires the container to be in a joinable netns state. In `iol.Start`, `disableMgmtTxOffload` runs right after `n.DefaultNode.Start(ctx)` which calls `StartContainer` — the container is running. Good.

But wait — there's a subtlety for `disableMgmtTxOffload` being called during `Start`: in the Start path, the container was previously stopped. `ExecFunction` checks `ContainerHasJoinableNetns(st)` — running is fine.

Now, one more important check: during `deploy` (fresh deploy), for the linux kind, `Deploy` calls `StartContainer` and then `PostDeploy` calls `disableEth0TxOffload`. There's no `Start` call during fresh deploy (deployNode → Deploy → PostDeploy). So no double-exec.

For IOL during fresh deploy: `Deploy` (DefaultNode) → PostDeploy → disableMgmtTxOffload. Good.

Now, `restart` (`core/restart.go`) calls `node.Stop(ctx)` then `node.Start(ctx)` — no PostDeploy. So before this PR, `clab restart` on IOL/linux nodes would lose offload settings. The PR fixes that. Good, this is the bugfix motivation.

Now check `sonic` Start at sonic.go:156 for comparison — same pattern, so this PR follows an existing established pattern. Good — the PR is consistent with existing code.

Check sros.Start at 2278:

Reasoning
The PR follows the existing pattern established by `sros.Start` → `PostDeployEndpoints` and `sonic.Start` → `PostDeployEndpoints`. So the approach is consistent with the codebase.

Now, is there anything problematic?

1. `iol.Start`: One thing to verify — for the IOL node, when a node is started via `clab start`, `DefaultNode.Start` calls `RunExecFromConfig` which re-runs exec commands. The IOL-specific PostDeploy also calls `UpdateMgmtIntf` (reconfiguring mgmt IP on the serial console). After a `clab stop`/`clab start`, the IOL node's mgmt IP config inside the IOL process persists (NVRAM/config in memory?) — Stop uses SIGKILL by default? IOL's StopSignal? If IOL is killed, the IOS process loses its running config except saved... Well, that's out of scope; the PR only addresses the offload. Actually wait — hmm, could this be an inconsistency worth noting? The PR adds `Start` for IOL which only re-applies offload, but not `UpdateMgmtIntf`. If the IOL process restarts after stop/start, does it re-apply mgmt IP? IOL boots from boot_config.txt (mounted), which contains the mgmt IP as of last deploy. If mgmt IP was updated via UpdateMgmtIntf at deploy (not first boot) — it writes running config... Actually on restart, the container re-runs and re-reads the config, but the IP written by UpdateMgmtIntf at deploy time went into running config via console, and if saved (`wr` is in the command string) — the mgmt_str ends with "wr\r" so it writes to NVRAM. OK, out of scope.

2. Check `restartApplyNodes` — `node.Stop` then `node.Start`. For linux nodes with data-plane endpoints: Stop parks endpoints; Start restores. Fine.

3. Now let me check ` redeploy` (core/deploy.go line 672 path) — this calls `Start` for nodes in `startNodeSet` — nodes that already exist but stopped. Good.

4. Now verify the IOL comment claim "it is never parked" — the mgmt interface is not an endpoint so it's not parked; on stop, the runtime deletes the container's netns → mgmt veth gone → recreated on start. Accurate.

5. Any issue with `disableMgmtTxOffload` returning early on error in the new helper vs base behavior? Base: on VethPeerIndex error → log warn, skip DisableTxOffload. Head: same (return after warn). Identical.

6. Check whether there's a test file for linux/iol Start. Let me glob for iol tests and linux tests.

7. Also check docs: `docs/manual/kinds/cisco_iol.md` — does it mention the TX offload behavior? If the PR introduces a behavior change visible to users (offload now re-applied on start), maybe a note is warranted. Minor.

8. Also check `cmd/tools_disableTxOffload.go` — the manual tool still exists; fine.

/source/head/docs/cmd/deploy.md:
  Line 331: - Disabling TX checksum offload


/source/head/docs/cmd/tools/disable-tx-offload.md:
  Line 1: # disable-tx-offload command

  Line 5: The `disable-tx-offload` command under the `tools` command disables tx checksum offload for `eth0` interface of a container referenced by its name.

  Line 7: The need for `disable-tx-offload` might arise when you launch a container outside of containerlab or restart a container. Some nodes, like SR Linux, will require correct checksums in TCP packets; thus, it is needed to disable checksum offload on those containers to do checksum calculations instead of offloading it.

  Line 11: `containerlab tools disable-tx-offload [local-flags]`

  Line 16: With the local mandatory `--container | -c` flag, a user specifies which container to remove tx offload.

  Line 21: # disable tx checksum offload on gnmic container

  Line 22: ❯ clab tools disable-tx-offload -c clab-st-gnmic

  Line 24: INFO[0000] Tx checksum offload disabled for eth0 interface of clab-st-gnmic container 


/source/head/docs/rn/0.44.md:
  Line 41: * fix: TCP sessions on srlinux were not establishing due to missing disabled tx offload #1537


/source/head/docs/rn/0.74.md:
  Line 39: ## TX offload on clab network

  Line 41: The long-standing code that disabled TX checksum offload on the Containerlab management bridge has been removed. This code was originally added to work around checksum issues with SR Linux, but since then SR Linux started recomputing checksums and the per-veth offload disable (which remains in place) is the correct fix. The stale bridge-level disable was causing DNS failures for containers running under Orbstack and potentially other hypervisors. #3082

  Line 63: - Fixed distributed SR-SIM nodes becoming unreachable when deployed with `--skip-post-deploy` by moving TX offload disable to `DeployEndpoints()`. Also fixed `PostDeploy` timeout loop that never iterated. Thanks @bayars #3045

  Line 64: - Disabled TX offload on IOL node management interface, fixing SSH connectivity from other devices on the bridge. Thanks @bendergm #3077


/source/head/docs/rn/0.15.md:
  Line 64: * Fixed disabling tx checksum offload for linux nodes [#543](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/issues/543)

  Line 65: * Fixed `tools disable-tx-offload` command [#543](https://github.com/srl-labs/containerlab/issues/543)


/source/head/docs/rn/0.72.md:
  Line 59: - Disabled TX checksum offload on host-side veth for SR-SIM management interfaces #2932


/source/head/docs/rn/0.12.0.md:
  Line 75: 5. If the changes containerlab makes to LLDP/TX-offload on the management bridge fail, they won't prevent the lab from proceed deploying.

<path>/source/head/docs/cmd/deploy.md</path>

<content>
318: ///
319: 
320: #### skip-post-deploy
321: 
322: The `--skip-post-deploy` flag skips the post-deploy phase of the lab deployment, affecting all nodes.
323: 
324: The post-deploy phase runs after containers and network endpoints are created. Depending on the node kind, it may include:
325: 
326: - Readiness and health checks
327: - TLS certificate provisioning
328: - Saving startup configuration
329: - Applying overlay CLI configuration
330: - Populating `/etc/hosts` with peer node entries
331: - Disabling TX checksum offload
332: 
333: Node kinds with notable post-deploy actions include Nokia SR Linux, Nokia SR OS, Arista cEOS, Juniper cRPD, Linux, and vrnetlab-based nodes. Kinds without a post-deploy phase are unaffected by this flag.
334: 
335: This flag is useful to bypass post-deploy validation failures or to speed up deployment when only the running containers are needed.
336: 
337: #### skip-labdir-acl
338: 
339: The `--skip-labdir-acl` flag can be used to skip the lab directory access control list (ACL) provisioning.
340: 
341: The extended File ACLs are provisioned for the lab directory by default, unless this flag is set. Extended File ACLs allow a sudo user to access the files in the lab directory that might be created by the `root` user from within the container node.
342: 
343: While this is useful in most cases, sometimes extended File ACLs might prevent your lab from working, especially when your lab directory end up being mounted from the network filesystem (NFS, CIFS, etc.). In such cases, you can use this flag to skip the ACL provisioning.
344: 
345: #### no-topology-cr
346: 
347: The `--no-topology-cr` flag applies to the [clabernetes runtime](../manual/clabernetes/runtime.md) only. By default, deploy persists the rendered topology as a c9s `Topology` resource and the Clabernetes manager compiles it into the `Node`, `Link`, and `NodeProfile` resources. With this flag, containerlab skips the `Topology` resource and compiles the topology client-side, creating and reconciling those resources directly.

(Showing lines 318-347 of 565. Use offset=348 to continue.)
</content>

85: Do not try to remove the management network during destroy phase. Usually the management docker network (in case of docker) and the underlying bridge are being removed. If you have attached additional resources outside of containerlab and you want the bridge to remain intact just add the `--keep-mgmt-net` flag.
86: 
87: #### skip-post-deploy
88: 
89: The `--skip-post-deploy` flag skips the post-deploy phase of the lab deployment, affecting all nodes.
90: 
91: The post-deploy phase runs after containers and network endpoints are created. Depending on the node kind, it may include:
92: 
93: - Readiness and health checks
94: - TLS certificate provisioning
95: - Saving startup configuration
96: - Applying overlay CLI configuration
97: - Populating `/etc/hosts` with peer node entries
98: - Disabling TX checksum offload
99: 
100: Node kinds with notable post-deploy actions include Nokia SR Linux, Nokia SR OS, Arista cEOS, Juniper cRPD, Linux, and vrnetlab-based nodes. Kinds without a post-deploy phase are unaffected by this flag.
101: 
102: This flag is useful to bypass post-deploy validation failures or to speed up redeployment when only the running containers are needed.
103: 
104: #### export-template
105: 
106: The local `--export-template` flag allows a user to specify a custom Go template that will be used for exporting topology data into `topology-data.json` file under the lab directory.
107: 
108: #### skip-labdir-acl
109: 
110: The `--skip-labdir-acl` flag can be used to skip the lab directory access control list (ACL) provisioning during the deploy phase.
111: 
112: #### no-topology-cr
113: 
114: The `--no-topology-cr` flag applies to the [clabernetes runtime](../manual/clabernetes/runtime.md) only. It makes the deploy phase skip the c9s `Topology` resource and create the compiled `Node`, `Link`, and `NodeProfile` resources directly. See [`deploy --no-topology-cr`](deploy.md#no-topology-cr).

So no release note was added for this fix. Per repo convention (previous similar fixes were noted in rn, e.g., 0.74.md line 64 for the IOL offload fix), a release note entry would be appropriate. That's a P4/P3 docs finding? The rule says "inspect any changes under docs/rn/" — there are none, so the rule doesn't directly flag. But documentation check #4 says user-visible behavior changes should be reflected in docs. The behavior change: `clab start`/`clab restart` now re-applies TX offload fix for `cisco_iol` and `linux` kinds. This is user-visible (fixes broken connectivity after restart). A release note entry in the next unreleased rn (0.82?) would be the convention. Hmm, but is there an unreleased rn file? Latest is 0.81. Let me check git — can't. I'll suggest adding a release note entry as a P4 (optional) or P3. Given repo convention, I'd make it a P4 docs suggestion... Actually check: does the repo require rn entries? Let me check .cursor/rules/git-mutations.mdc quickly and whether recent fixes include rn. The rn 0.74 line 64 shows the IOL fix got a rn entry. So convention exists. I'll flag as P4.

Now, deeper correctness concerns to verify:

A. `iol.Start` — does IOL node get its `firstBoot` etc.? Not relevant.

B. Critical check: `disableMgmtTxOffload` uses `n.Runtime.Mgmt().Driver` — is `n.Runtime` set when `Start` is invoked via `core/start.go`? Yes, loaded from topology.

C. Another critical check: In `core/start.go` `startNodesWithLabRuntime` path — does it call node.Start? Yes.

D. Timing issue in `iol.Start`: `VethPeerIndex` is executed via `ExecFunction` which enters the container's netns. Right after `StartContainer`, the netns should exist. But for `DefaultNode.Start`, `StartContainer` waits for running? Let me check the runtime StartContainer — it typically waits until running. OK.

E. Now the potential double-application during `clab deploy` when node was already running? deployNodes only calls Start for startNodeSet (stopped nodes). Not an issue.

F. What about `linux` nodes with `network-mode: none` — no eth0? Then ExecFunction NSEthtoolTXOff fails → `log.Error(err)` (not fatal) — same as base PostDeploy. On Start, logs an error. Pre-existing behavior; Start now logs error too, harmless.

Hmm wait, actually for network-mode none linux nodes: CheckInterfaceName allows eth0 only with network-mode none. For none mode, there is no eth0. PostDeploy previously attempted and failed with log.Error. So identical on Start. Fine.

G. `linux.Start` vs `DefaultNode.Start` ordering: `RunExecFromConfig` runs inside DefaultNode.Start before `disableEth0TxOffload`. If a user's exec configures eth0 addresses, offload disable after is fine.

H. Concurrency: `restartApplyNodes` is sequential; deployNodes workers call Start concurrently for different nodes — each touches own netns. Fine.

I. Is there any issue with the IOL Start not calling `GenBootConfig`? Not needed.

J. What about `iol.Start` missing the `UpdateMgmtIntf` behavior that PostDeploy does? On stop/start, the IOL process is killed (StopSignal?). IOL config: on stop, the running config is lost unless saved; NVRAM persists saved config. The mgmt IP was written to NVRAM via `wr` in UpdateMgmtIntf. So on start, IOL boots with saved config containing the mgmt IP. Consistent.

K. What about `restartApplyNodes` used by `clab restart` — for IOL, after stop, dataplane endpoints parked; start restores. But does IOL's iouyap re-attach to restored endpoints? Out of scope.

L. One more: the linux kind Start comment says "the runtime recreates the mgmt veth (it is never parked)". For the linux kind, eth0 is the mgmt interface, and disableEth0TxOffload disables offload inside the container netns on eth0 (the container side of the mgmt veth). But is eth0 recreated? On container start, yes.

But hold on — for the linux kind, `disableEth0TxOffload` disables TX checksum offload on the container-side eth0. When a container is stopped and started, eth0 is recreated → default on. So re-apply needed. Correct.

M. Potential gap (worth a P4 note?): The same stop/start offload reset presumably affects other kinds with the same problem — e.g., `tools disable-tx-offload` doc line 7 says "restart a container". But the PR only fixes iol and linux kinds. sros already handles Start via PostDeployEndpoints. So consistent coverage now. Good.

N. Check that `iol` struct's `Start` doesn't break other lifecycle callers: `default_node.go:442` — `d.Start(ctx)`? Let me look at line 430-450 context — probably in a deploy helper. Actually line 442: `if err := d.Start(ctx); err != nil {` — inside what function? Let me check. This could be `WithStart` or a deploy path where PostDeploy is also called → double disable (idempotent, harmless).

Let me check that context.

func (n *linux) PostDeploy(...) error {
	log.Debugf(...)
	err := n.ExecFunction(ctx, clabutils.NSEthtoolTXOff(n.GetShortName(), "eth0"))

}

1: # start command
2: <!-- --8<-- [start:lifecycle-commands] -->
3: The node's lifecycle command set consists of [start](start.md), [stop](stop.md), and [restart](restart.md) commands that allow users to individually control the lifecycle of the nodes in a deployed lab. The most common use case is to restart or stop+start the nodes that are stuck in some bad shape or require a reboot while keeping their dataplane interfaces intact.
4: 
5: With these commands you don't need to respin the whole topology when all you need is to restart a few nodes.
6: 
7: <!-- --8<-- [end:lifecycle-commands] -->
8: 
9: ### Description
10: 
11: The `start` command starts one or more stopped nodes in a deployed lab and restores dataplane
12: interfaces parked by `containerlab stop`.
13: 
14: This command is intended to be used with nodes previously stopped by containerlab lifecycle
15: operations.
16: 
17: --8<-- "docs/cmd/deploy.md:env-vars-flags"
18: 
19: ### Usage
20: 
21: `containerlab [global-flags] start [local-flags]`
22: 
23: ### Flags
24: 
25: #### topology | name
26: 
27: Use the global `--topo | -t` flag to reference the lab topology file, or use the global `--name`
28: flag to reference an already deployed lab by name.
29: 
30: One of `--topo` or `--name` is required.
31: 
32: #### node
33: 
34: Use local `--node | -n` to select nodes to start.
35: 
36: The flag is repeatable and also supports comma-separated values:
37: 
38: ```bash
39: containerlab start -t mylab.clab.yml --node r1 --node r2
40: containerlab start -t mylab.clab.yml --node r1,r2
41: ```
42: 
43: If `--node` is omitted, all nodes in the selected lab are started.
44: 
45: ### Limitations
46: 
47: <!-- --8<-- [start:limitations] -->
48: Node lifecycle operations (`stop`, `start`, `restart`) currently support only a subset of nodes:
49: 
50: - only veth dataplane links are supported
51: - root-namespace-based nodes are not supported
52: - nodes with `auto-remove` enabled are not supported
53: - only single-container nodes are supported
54: - `network-mode: container:<...>` users/providers are not supported
55: <!-- --8<-- [end:limitations] -->
56: 
57: ### Examples
58: 
59: #### Start a single node by topology file
60: 
61: ```bash
62: containerlab start -t mylab.clab.yml --node r1
63: ```
64: 
65: #### Start multiple nodes by lab name
66: 
67: ```bash
68: containerlab start --name mylab --node r1,r2
69: ```
70: 
71: #### Start all nodes in a lab
72: 
73: ```bash
74: containerlab start -t mylab.clab.yml
75: containerlab start --name mylab
76: ```

1: # restart command
2: 
3: --8<-- "docs/cmd/start.md:lifecycle-commands"
4: 
5: ### Description
6: 
7: The `restart` command restarts one or more nodes in a deployed lab by performing a lifecycle-aware
8: stop+start operation.
9: 
10: For running nodes, containerlab parks dataplane interfaces before stop and restores them on start.
11: For already stopped nodes, containerlab performs the start/restore phase.
12: 
13: --8<-- "docs/cmd/deploy.md:env-vars-flags"
14: 
15: ### Usage
16: 
17: `containerlab [global-flags] restart [local-flags]`
18: 
19: ### Flags
20: 
21: #### topology | name
22: 
23: Use the global `--topo | -t` flag to reference the lab topology file, or use the global `--name`
24: flag to reference an already deployed lab by name.
25: 
26: One of `--topo` or `--name` is required.
27: 
28: #### node
29: 
30: Use local `--node | -n` to select nodes to restart.
31: 
32: The flag is repeatable and also supports comma-separated values:
33: 
34: ```bash
35: containerlab restart -t mylab.clab.yml --node r1 --node r2
36: containerlab restart -t mylab.clab.yml --node r1,r2
37: ```
38: 
39: If `--node` is omitted, all nodes in the selected lab are restarted.
40: 
41: ### Limitations
42: 
43: --8<-- "docs/cmd/start.md:limitations"
44: 
45: ### Examples
46: 
47: #### Restart a single node by topology file
48: 
49: ```bash
50: containerlab restart -t mylab.clab.yml --node r1
51: ```
52: 
53: #### Restart multiple nodes by lab name
54: 
55: ```bash
56: containerlab restart --name mylab --node r1,r2
57: ```
58: 
59: #### Restart all nodes in a lab
60: 
61: ```bash
62: containerlab restart -t mylab.clab.yml
63: containerlab restart --name mylab
64: ```

Docs don't contradict. Behavior change is a pure fix; a release note entry is repo convention (rn 0.74 has similar entries). This is P4.

Now, is there an unreleased rn file where this should go? Latest is 0.81. Let me check whether 0.81 is released — can't know. I'll suggest "the next unreleased release note".

Now, one more thing to check: `iol.Start` — does the IOL node need `RunExecFromConfig`? It's in DefaultNode.Start. OK.

Another check: `disableMgmtTxOffload` for IOL only handles the mgmt interface (eth0). IOL's data-plane interfaces are bridged by iouyap with packet sockets from inside the container. Do dataplane veths have the same offload problem after restart? The original fix (0.74 rn: "Disabled TX offload on IOL node management interface, fixing SSH connectivity from other devices on the bridge") only handled mgmt. So data-plane is unchanged by this PR. OK.

Now potential P3/P2 findings:

1. Duplicate pattern with `sros.PostDeployEndpoints`: IOL's `disableMgmtTxOffload` is nearly identical to `sros.PostDeployEndpoints` (macvlan guard + VethPeerIndex + DisableTxOffloadByIndex). Per Ponytail rung 2 ("Does it already exist in this codebase? Reuse the helper") — but these are in different packages with kind-specific logging. Could move to `utils` as a shared helper taking a node interface. It's a small duplication (2 packages, now 3 if counting sros). P4 at most. Hmm — actually, careful: I shouldn't demand abstraction. sros's version returns `nil` after warn; iol's returns after warn; identical structure but different log text. I'll mention as P4 optional.

Actually wait — should I even flag it? The instruction: "Prefer a small correction at the root cause; do not recommend an abstraction merely to remove superficially similar code." The two functions are superficially similar but with different log messages and contexts. I'll skip it or note it in coverage. I'll skip as a finding and mention in coverage notes.

2. Missing test: The changed behavior (`Start` re-applies offload) has no unit test. But testing requires a real runtime/container; `iol.Start` and `linux.Start` are thin wrappers around runtime-dependent calls. Hard to unit test meaningfully. The smoke tests / kind-specific CI workflows (`cisco_iol-tests.yml`) exist. Let me check whether cisco_iol-tests.yml covers stop/start. If not, mention in coverage.

3. Documentation: behavior fix isn't documented anywhere user-facing. P4.

4. One genuine question: in `linux.Start`, the `disableEth0TxOffload` call happens even for nodes where the container just started — fine.

5. Another possible issue: `iol.Start` — PostDeploy calls `disableMgmtTxOffload` BEFORE `GenBootConfig`/`UpdateMgmtIntf`. In Start path there's no UpdateMgmtIntf; if the mgmt IP changed since deploy... not applicable.

6. Wait — one real consideration: on `clab start` after `clab stop` for IOL, `DefaultNode.Start` calls `RunExecFromConfig`, but IOL's mgmt IP inside the IOS process: after a hard stop (StopSignal?), the IOL process loses running config; it boots from NVRAM saved config (written via `wr` during UpdateMgmtIntf). If the user never triggered save, saved config is from... the boot config. Acceptable.

7. Check `ContainerHasJoinableNetns(st)` — if the container status after `StartContainer` is Running, fine.

8. Now, one more important check: does the runtime `StartContainer` for IOL do something async so that eth0 might not exist yet? StartContainer blocks until running. The netns exists when container starts. eth0 is added by the runtime when creating the container (mgmt network connect) — present at start. Fine.

Now let me double check the cisco_iol-tests.yml workflow to see if stop/start is covered.
